# Next.js 16.3.8 — Static Portfolio/Marketing Site Cheat Sheet

All paths relative to `node_modules/next/dist/docs/`. Version verified: `next@16.3.8`.

---

## 1. Breaking changes 16 vs 15/14
Source: `01-app/02-guides/upgrading/version-16.md`

**Dynamic route params / searchParams (Async Request APIs)**
- "Starting with **Next.js 16**, synchronous access is fully removed." Applies to `cookies`, `headers`, `draftMode`, `params` in `layout.js`/`page.js`/`route.js`/`default.js`/`opengraph-image`/`twitter-image`/`icon`/`apple-icon`, and `searchParams` in `page.js`. You must `await` / `use()`.
- Type helpers generated by `npx next typegen`: `PageProps`, `LayoutProps`, `RouteContext` (globally available, no import).
- Codemod: `npx @next/codemod@canary next-async-request-api .`

**Metadata**
- "the image generating function now receives `params` and `id` as promises" for `opengraph-image`, `twitter-image`, `icon`, `apple-icon`. `generateImageMetadata` still receives **synchronous** `params`.
- `sitemap`: `const resolvedId = await id // id is now Promise<string>`.

**Images** (`next/image` section, L660–905)
| Change | Detail |
|---|---|
| Local images w/ query strings | need `images.localPatterns.search` config, else blocked |
| `images.minimumCacheTTL` | default `60s` → `14400s` (4h) |
| `images.imageSizes` | `16` removed from default array |
| `images.qualities` | default all → `[75]` (values outside are coerced to nearest) |
| Local IP optimization | blocked by default; `dangerouslyAllowLocalIP` |
| `images.maximumRedirects` | unlimited → `3` |
| `next/legacy/image` | **deprecated** → use `next/image` |
| `images.domains` | **deprecated** → `images.remotePatterns` |

**Fonts:** no breaking changes in the v16 upgrade guide (grep confirms zero `font` hits). `01-app/03-api-reference/02-components/font.md` version table only lists v13.x changes.

**Config**
- `next lint` **removed**; `next build` no longer lints; `eslint` key removed from `next.config.*`.
- `serverRuntimeConfig` / `publicRuntimeConfig` **removed** → env vars / `NEXT_PUBLIC_`.
- `experimental.turbopack` → top-level `turbopack`.
- `skipMiddlewareUrlNormalize` → `skipProxyUrlNormalize`.
- `experimental.dynamicIO` / `experimental.useCache` removed → `cacheComponents: true`.
- `unstable_rootParams` removed → `next/root-params`.
- AMP removed; `devIndicators` loses `appIsrStatus`/`buildActivity`/`buildActivityPosition`.
- Route Segment Config: "`v16.0.0` `dynamic`, `dynamicParams`, `revalidate`, and `fetchCache` removed when Cache Components is enabled"; `export const experimental_ppr` removed.

**Dev server / bundler**
- "Starting with **Next.js 16**, Turbopack is stable and used by default with `next dev` and `next build`". Custom `webpack` config makes build **fail**; escape hatches: `next build --turbopack` or `--webpack`.
- `next dev` outputs to `.next/dev`; lockfile prevents concurrent instances; config loads once (`process.argv` no longer contains `'dev'`).
- Build output drops `size` and `First Load JS` metrics ("use Lighthouse or Vercel Analytics").

**Client components**
- React **19.2** canary (`ViewTransition`, `useEffectEvent`, `Activity`); React Compiler stable but **opt-in**: `reactCompiler: true`.
- `serverRuntimeConfig` removal affects client config (use `NEXT_PUBLIC_`).

**Link / prefetch**
- "Enhanced Routing and Navigation": layout dedup + incremental prefetching — "**no code modifications**"; expect "more individual prefetch requests with much lower total transfer sizes".
- New in 16.2: `<Link transitionTypes>`; `partialPrefetching` added in 16.3 (requires `cacheComponents`).

**Middleware → proxy**
- `middleware.ts` filename **deprecated**, rename to `proxy.ts`; named export `middleware` → `proxy`. "`edge` runtime is **NOT** supported in `proxy`… runtime is `nodejs`, and it cannot be configured." Keep `middleware` if you need `edge`.

**Other**: Node.js ≥ `20.9.0`, TypeScript ≥ `5.1.0`; parallel-route slots **require** `default.js` (build fails); global smooth-scroll no longer overridden — add `data-scroll-behavior="smooth"` to `<html>` to restore.

---

## 2. Dynamic routes — `app/projects/[slug]/page.tsx`

**`params` IS a Promise.** Exact shape (from `01-app/03-api-reference/03-file-conventions/page.md`):

```tsx
export default async function Page({
  params,
}: {
  params: Promise<{ slug: string }>
}) {
  const { slug } = await params
}
```
Route table: `app/shop/[slug]/page.js` → `Promise<{ slug: '1' }>`; catch-all → `Promise<{ slug: ['1','2'] }>`.

`searchParams`: `Promise<{ [key: string]: string | string[] | undefined }>` — "a **Request-time API**… Using it will opt the page into **dynamic rendering**". Plain object, not `URLSearchParams`.

Client page: `const { slug } = use(params)` (`use` from `react`), or `useParams()`.

Typed helper (`page.md` §Page Props Helper):
```tsx
export default async function Page(props: PageProps<'/blog/[slug]'>) {
  const { slug } = await props.params
  const query = await props.searchParams
}
```
Types are generated by `next dev`, `next build`, or `next typegen`; no import needed.

**`generateStaticParams`** (`01-app/03-api-reference/04-functions/generate-static-params.md`):
```tsx
export async function generateStaticParams() {
  const posts = await fetch('https://.../posts').then((res) => res.json())
  return posts.map((post) => ({ slug: post.slug }))
}
```
- Params: `options.params` (optional, parent segment's values).
- Returns: "an array of objects" — `/product/[id]` → `{ id: string }[]`.
- Works in `page`, `layout`, `route`. Runs once per build; not re-run on ISR revalidation.
- Companion: `export const dynamicParams = true // true | false` (`02-route-segment-config/dynamicParams.md`) — `false` → 404 for unknown slugs. "not available when Cache Components is enabled."

**`generateMetadata`** (`04-functions/generate-metadata.md`):
```tsx
import type { Metadata, ResolvingMetadata } from 'next'

type Props = {
  params: Promise<{ id: string }>
  searchParams: Promise<{ [key: string]: string | string[] | undefined }>
}

export async function generateMetadata(
  { params, searchParams }: Props,
  parent: ResolvingMetadata
): Promise<Metadata> {
  const { id } = await params
  const previousImages = (await parent).openGraph?.images || []
  return { title: '...', openGraph: { images: ['/og.png', ...previousImages] } }
}
```
Rules: Server Components only; "You cannot export both the `metadata` object and `generateMetadata` from the same route segment"; prefer static `metadata` "If metadata doesn't depend on request information"; file-based metadata wins over both.

---

## 3. Metadata & file conventions

**Static export** (`import type { Metadata } from 'next'`):
```tsx
export const metadata: Metadata = {
  title: { template: '%s | Acme', default: 'Acme' }, // template requires default
  description: 'The React Framework for the Web',
  metadataBase: new URL('https://acme.com'),
  alternates: { canonical: '/', languages: { 'en-US': '/en-US' },
                media: { 'only screen and (max-width: 600px)': '/mobile' },
                types: { 'application/rss+xml': 'https://acme.com/rss' } },
  openGraph: { title: 'Next.js', description: '...', url: 'https://nextjs.org',
               siteName: 'Next.js', locale: 'en_US', type: 'website',
               images: [{ url: 'https://…/og.png', width: 800, height: 600 }] },
  twitter: { card: 'summary_large_image', title: '...', description: '...',
             creator: '@nextjs', images: ['https://…/og.png'] },
}
```
- "Using a relative path in a URL-based `metadata` field without configuring a `metadataBase` will cause a build error."
- `title.default` / `title.template` / `title.absolute`; `themeColor` and `colorScheme` are **deprecated since v14** → use `generateViewport`.

**`opengraph-image.tsx`** (`01-app/03-api-reference/03-file-conventions/01-metadata/opengraph-image.md`) — **import is `next/og`**:
```tsx
import { ImageResponse } from 'next/og'
export const alt = 'About Acme'
export const size = { width: 1200, height: 630 }
export const contentType = 'image/png'

export default async function Image() {
  return new ImageResponse(<div style={{ display: 'flex' }}>About Acme</div>, {
    ...size,
    fonts: [{ name: 'Inter', data: interSemiBold, style: 'normal', weight: 400 }],
  })
}
```
`ImageResponse` ctor (`04-functions/image-response.md`): `new ImageResponse(element: ReactElement, options: { width?=1200, height?=630, emoji?='twemoji', fonts?, debug?, status?=200, statusText?, headers? })`. Limits: 500KB bundle, flexbox-only CSS, fonts `ttf|otf|woff`. Static file variants: `opengraph-image.(jpg|jpeg|png|gif)` + `opengraph-image.alt.txt` (OG ≤ 8MB, Twitter ≤ 5MB or build fails).

**`sitemap.ts`**:
```ts
import type { MetadataRoute } from 'next'
export default function sitemap(): MetadataRoute.Sitemap {
  return [{ url: 'https://acme.com', lastModified: new Date(),
            changeFrequency: 'yearly', priority: 1 }]
}
```
**`robots.ts`**:
```ts
import type { MetadataRoute } from 'next'
export default function robots(): MetadataRoute.Robots {
  return { rules: { userAgent: '*', allow: '/', disallow: '/private/' },
           sitemap: 'https://acme.com/sitemap.xml' }
}
```
Both are "special Route Handler[s]… cached by default."

**`not-found.tsx`** (`03-file-conventions/not-found.md`): default-export `NotFound()`; renders between `loading.js` and `page.js`; returns `404` non-streamed, `200` when streamed (streamed HTML gets `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">`). Optional experimental `global-not-found.js` via `experimental.globalNotFound`.

---

## 4. Fonts (`01-app/03-api-reference/02-components/font.md`)

```tsx
import { Inter } from 'next/font/google'
const inter = Inter({ subsets: ['latin'], display: 'swap', variable: '--font-inter' })
// <html lang="en" className={`${inter.variable} antialiased`}>
```
Option table (google / local): `src` (local only), `weight`, `style`, `subsets`, `axes`, `display`, `preload`, `fallback`, `adjustFontFallback`, `variable`, `declarations` (local only).
- `weight`: `'400'` | range `'100 900'` | array `['400','700']` (array = non-variable google font only). "Required if the font being used is **not** variable."
- `display`: `'auto' | 'block' | 'swap' | 'fallback' | 'optional'`, default `'swap'`.
- `preload` default `true`; "Failing to specify any subsets while `preload` is `true` will result in a warning."
- `adjustFontFallback` default `true` (google) / `'Arial'` (local).
- Multi-word fonts use underscore: `Roboto_Mono`.
- Returned members: `.className`, `.style`, `.variable`.

**Tailwind v4 wiring** (exact snippet from `font.md`):
```css
@import 'tailwindcss';

@theme inline {
  --font-sans: var(--font-inter);
  --font-mono: var(--font-roboto-mono);
}
```
(Tailwind v3 fallback: `theme.extend.fontFamily.sans = ['var(--font-inter)']`.) Tailwind v4 install per `01-app/01-getting-started/11-css.md`: `npm i -D tailwindcss @tailwindcss/postcss` + `postcss.config` with `'@tailwindcss/postcss': {}`.
**Deprecations: none.** Version table stops at `v13.2.0 @next/font renamed to next/font`.

---

## 5. Images (`01-app/03-api-reference/02-components/image.md`)

Props: `src`, `alt` (required), `width`/`height`, `fill`, `loader`, `sizes`, `quality`, **`preload`**, `placeholder`, `style`, `onLoad`, `onError`, `loading`, `blurDataURL`, `unoptimized`, `overrideSrc`, `decoding`. Deprecated: `onLoadingComplete` (v14).

- **`priority` is deprecated in Next 16**: "Starting with Next.js 16, the `priority` property has been deprecated in favor of the [`preload`](#preload) property". `preload={true}` inserts a `<link>` in `<head>` — use for LCP/hero. Docs add: "In most cases, you should use `loading='eager'` or `fetchPriority='high'` instead of `preload`."
- `loading` default `lazy`; `quality` default `75`.
- `placeholder`: `'empty'` (default) | `'blur'` (needs `blurDataURL`) | `data:image/...`. Static import of `jpg|png|webp|avif` auto-provides `width`, `height`, `blurDataURL`.
- `sizes`: "If `sizes` is missing, the browser assumes the image will be as wide as the viewport (`100vw`)"; required with `fill`/responsive CSS.
- Config: `formats: ['image/webp']` (default); `['image/avif','image/webp']` for AVIF; `qualities: [75]` default (doc: "required starting with Next.js 16"); `remotePatterns` (NOT `domains`).

**Local `/public` images:** no config needed — `01-getting-started/12-images.md` §Local images just uses `<Image src="/profile.png" … width={500} height={500} />`. Only if the `src` carries a **query string** do you need `images.localPatterns`. Note `03-file-conventions/public-folder.md`: public assets ship `Cache-Control: public, max-age=0` — prefer static imports (immutable, hashed) for LCP.

---

## 6. Client/server boundary — `next/dynamic`

Source: `01-app/02-guides/lazy-loading.md`. **`ssr: false` is allowed only inside a Client Component.** Exact documented pattern:

```jsx
'use client'
import dynamic from 'next/dynamic'

const ComponentC = dynamic(() => import('../components/C'), { ssr: false })
```
Documented errors: "`ssr: false` option is not supported in Server Components. You will see an error if you try to use it in Server Components." / "`ssr: false` is not allowed with `next/dynamic` in Server Components. Please move it into a Client Component." / "When a Server Component dynamically imports a Client Component, automatic code splitting is currently **not** supported."

Custom fallback: `dynamic(() => import('../components/X'), { loading: () => <p>Loading...</p> })`. Named export: `dynamic(() => import('../components/hello').then((mod) => mod.Hello))`. Also new in 16: magic comments `turbopackIgnore`, `turbopackOptional` (no `webpackOptional` under Turbopack).

**`loading.tsx`** (`03-file-conventions/loading.md`): default export, **no params**; Server Component by default; auto-wraps `not-found.js`, `page.js`, nested `layout.js` in `<Suspense>` (does NOT wrap same-segment `layout.js`/`template.js`/`error.js`). Caveat: "If the layout accesses uncached or runtime data… `loading.js` will not show a fallback for it." Manual Suspense:
```tsx
<Suspense fallback={<p>Loading feed...</p>}><PostFeed /></Suspense>
```

---

## 7. Dev/build & `next.config.ts`

- **Turbopack is default** for both `next dev` and `next build` (no `--turbopack` needed). Opt out: `next build --webpack`.
- `typedRoutes: true` — "**Note**: This option has been marked as stable, so you should use `typedRoutes` instead of `experimental.typedRoutes`."
- `reactStrictMode` — "Since Next.js 13.5.1, Strict Mode is `true` by default with `app` router… You can still disable Strict Mode by setting `reactStrictMode: false`."
- **`cacheComponents` is opt-in, default OFF**: "To enable the `cacheComponents` flag, set it to `true` in your `next.config.ts` file." For a mostly-static site you do **not** need it; without it, the previous caching model (`caching-without-cache-components.md`, `revalidate`/`fetchCache` segment configs) still applies. `experimental.dynamicIO`/`useCache` are gone — if unused, "remove the flags instead."
- `partialPrefetching: true` (16.3) **requires** `cacheComponents` or the config validator throws.
- Turbopack config is top-level: `const nextConfig: NextConfig = { turbopack: { resolveAlias: { … } } }`.
- No static-ness knob needed: pages without Request-time APIs prerender automatically (`○ (Static)` in build output).

---

## 8. Must-follow for Lighthouse 90+

**`01-app/02-guides/production-checklist.md`**
- Server Components by default (zero client JS) + place `"use client"` boundaries carefully.
- "Request-time APIs like `cookies` and the `searchParams` prop will opt the entire route into Dynamic Rendering… Ensure Request-time API usage is intentional and wrap them in `<Suspense>`."
- Use `<Link>` (not `<a>`) for internal nav; prefetch on by default in production.
- **Font Module** ("reduces layout shift") + **`<Image>`** ("prevents layout shift, serves WebP") + **`<Script>`** for third-party.
- `public/` for static assets; `app/global-error.tsx`; `app/global-not-found.tsx`; Metadata API + OG images + sitemap/robots.
- `.env.*` in `.gitignore`, only `NEXT_PUBLIC_` exposed.
- Run Lighthouse in incognito; `useReportWebVitals`; `@next/bundle-analyzer`.

**`01-app/02-guides/public-static-pages.md`** (the canonical static-page recipe)
1. Static component → prerendered automatically, no config: "Notice that the product route is marked as static, even though we didn't add any explicit configuration."
2. Uncached `await` outside Suspense triggers warning `blocking-prerender-dynamic`. Fix: cache it with `'use cache'` inside the async component, **or** stream it.
3. Truly request-dependent data → wrap in `<Suspense fallback={<Skeleton />}>` for partial prerendering (`◐ (Partial Prerender)`).
4. Layout tip (from `dynamic-routes.md`): "In layouts, avoid awaiting `params` at the top level… pass the params promise down."

**`01-app/02-guides/lazy-loading.md`**: lazy-load heavy client components/libraries (`next/dynamic`, `import()` on demand) to cut initial JS.

## 9. Lint — `eslint-config-next` v16 (`01-app/03-api-reference/05-config/03-eslint.md`)

Flat config only; `next lint` and the `eslint` config option are **removed** (`v16.0.0` row). Canonical `eslint.config.mjs`:

```js
import { defineConfig, globalIgnores } from 'eslint/config'
import nextVitals from 'eslint-config-next/core-web-vitals'
import nextTs from 'eslint-config-next/typescript'   // TS projects

const eslintConfig = defineConfig([
  ...nextVitals,
  ...nextTs,
  globalIgnores(['.next/**', 'out/**', 'build/**', 'next-env.d.ts']),
])
export default eslintConfig
```
- Exports: `eslint-config-next`, `/core-web-vitals` (CWV rules → errors; default for new apps), `/typescript`.
- Run: `npx eslint .` — `next build` no longer lints.
- Prettier: add `eslint-config-prettier/flat`.
- Monorepo: `settings: { next: { rootDir: 'packages/my-app/' } }`.
- Alternative for conflicting setups: use `@next/eslint-plugin-next` directly with `...nextPlugin.configs.recommended.rules`.
</task_result>
</task>
```

1248 node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/02-guides/upgrading/version-16.md
---
title: How to upgrade to version 16
nav_title: Version 16
description: Upgrade your Next.js Application from Version 15 to 16.
---

{/* The content of this doc is shared between the app and pages router. You can use the `<PagesOnly>Content</PagesOnly>` component to add content that is specific to the Pages Router. Any shared content should not be wrapped in a component. */}

## Use an AI agent (recommended)

An AI coding agent can run the mechanical parts of the upgrade, inspect the diff, fix follow-up breakages, and verify the app. The prompt below asks the agent to read version-matched docs, use the codemods where appropriate, explain important changes, and verify the result. Some runtime verification tools are only available after the app is upgraded.

Give the agent this prompt:

```prompt
Upgrade this app to Next.js 16.

Before editing code, make sure AGENTS.md points at version-matched Next.js docs. If it is missing or outdated, follow [Set up AI agent docs](/docs/app/guides/upgrading/version-16#set-up-ai-agent-docs), then read AGENTS.md.

Then follow the [Next.js 16 upgrade guide](/docs/app/guides/upgrading/version-16) as the source of truth for the migration. Use the [codemod](/docs/app/guides/upgrading/version-16#using-the-codemod) when you're ready to run the mechanical upgrade.

Briefly explain the upgrade plan in user-facing language before making broad changes. Follow the documented defaults and keep moving unless the guide requires a project-specific decision, the change is destructive, credentials or environment setup are missing, or the correct migration is ambiguous. Keep the migration scoped to the upgrade, inspect the diff, run the relevant checks, and fix remaining breaking changes.

After the app is upgraded, use the runtime verification flow from the [AI Coding Agents guide](/docs/app/guides/ai-agents) to confirm it still works. Prefer the `next-dev-loop` skill when it is available (Next.js 16.3 or later with Turbopack); otherwise fall back to the best available `next dev`, browser, and build checks. Open the key interactive UI states and check the Next dev indicator plus browser and server logs. Summarize what changed, what was verified, and what could not be verified.

Before finishing, repeat the post-upgrade check in [Set up AI agent docs](/docs/app/guides/upgrading/version-16#set-up-ai-agent-docs) so the project is ready for future agent work.
```

## Or upgrade manually

Upgrading manually follows a few steps, in order:

1. [Set up AI agent docs](#set-up-ai-agent-docs) if you use an assistant now or want future agent work grounded in version-matched docs.
2. [Run the upgrade codemod](#using-the-codemod).
3. If you prefer not to run the codemod, [install packages manually](#install-packages-manually).
4. Continue through the breaking changes below, run the relevant checks, and fix any remaining issues.

## Set up AI agent docs

The prompt above asks the agent to do this before it edits code. You can also prepare the project yourself before starting the upgrade. See the [AI Coding Agents guide](/docs/app/guides/ai-agents) for the full setup.

```bash filename="Terminal"
npx @next/codemod@canary agents-md
```

After upgrading, verify `AGENTS.md` still points at the docs for the installed Next.js version. On Next.js 16.2 and later, that should be the bundled docs in `node_modules/next/dist/docs/`. If the pre-upgrade setup downloaded docs into `.next-docs/`, update `AGENTS.md` to point at the bundled docs, then remove `.next-docs/` once nothing references it.

The managed block should look like this:

```md filename="AGENTS.md"
<!-- BEGIN:nextjs-agent-rules -->

# This is NOT the Next.js you know

This version has breaking changes — APIs, conventions, and file structure may all differ from your training data. Read the relevant guide in `node_modules/next/dist/docs/` (resolved from this file's directory; in monorepos the `next` package may not be visible from the repo root) before writing any code. Heed deprecation notices.

This block is written and re-added by `next dev` — verify at `node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/generate-agent-files.js`. Removing it from a diff only re-creates the uncommitted change; committing it with your work keeps the tree clean.

<!-- END:nextjs-agent-rules -->
```

## Using the codemod

To update to Next.js version 16, you can use the `upgrade` [codemod](/docs/app/guides/upgrading/codemods#160):

```bash package="pnpm"
pnpm dlx @next/codemod@canary upgrade latest
```

```bash package="npm"
npx @next/codemod@canary upgrade latest
```

```bash package="yarn"
yarn dlx @next/codemod@canary upgrade latest
```

```bash package="bun"
bunx @next/codemod@canary upgrade latest
```

The [codemod](/docs/app/guides/upgrading/codemods#160) is able to:

- Update `next.config.js` to use the new `turbopack` configuration
- Migrate from `next lint` to the ESLint CLI
- Migrate from deprecated `middleware` convention to `proxy`
- Remove `unstable_` prefix from stabilized APIs
- Remove `experimental_ppr` Route Segment Config from pages and layouts

The `upgrade` codemod does not run every migration codemod. If your app still uses synchronous `params`, `searchParams`, `cookies()`, `headers()`, or `draftMode()` access from the Next.js 15 compatibility period, also run the async Request APIs codemod:

```bash filename="Terminal"
npx @next/codemod@canary next-async-request-api .
```

## Install packages manually

If you prefer to do it manually, install the latest Next.js and React versions:

```bash package="pnpm"
pnpm add next@latest react@latest react-dom@latest
```

```bash package="npm"
npm install next@latest react@latest react-dom@latest
```

```bash package="yarn"
yarn add next@latest react@latest react-dom@latest
```

```bash package="bun"
bun add next@latest react@latest react-dom@latest
```

If you are using TypeScript, ensure you also upgrade `@types/react` and `@types/react-dom` to their latest versions.

## Node.js runtime and browser support

| Requirement   | Change / Details                                                   |
| ------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Node.js 20.9+ | Minimum version now `20.9.0` (LTS); Node.js 18 no longer supported |
| TypeScript 5+ | Minimum version now `5.1.0`                                        |
| Browsers      | Chrome 111+, Edge 111+, Firefox 111+, Safari 16.4+                 |

## Turbopack by default

Starting with **Next.js 16**, Turbopack is stable and used by default with `next dev` and `next build`

Previously you had to enable Turbopack using `--turbopack`, or `--turbo`.

```json filename="package.json"
{
  "scripts": {
    "dev": "next dev --turbopack",
    "build": "next build --turbopack",
    "start": "next start"
  }
}
```

This is no longer necessary. You can update your `package.json` scripts:

```json filename="package.json"
{
  "scripts": {
    "dev": "next dev",
    "build": "next build",
    "start": "next start"
  }

```

  file: string;
  /** Exact file size in bytes */

  /** Human label for version */

}

  apk?: DownloadArtifact;
  windows?: DownloadArtifact;

'use strict';
/**
 * tests/e2e.js — two-player game flow (home → create → join → start → roll).
 *
 * Usage:  NODE_PATH=<playwright modules> node tests/e2e.js [baseurl]
 *         baseurl defaults to http://localhost:8131
 *
 * Prints a human-readable FAIL line for the first failing assertion, then a
 * pretty JSON object. Exits 0 only when every check passes (allPass === true).
 *
 * Determinism notes (no hard sleeps anywhere):
 *  - one fresh browser context per player (localStorage ludo-id differs → distinct players)
 *  - every wait is condition-based (waitForSelector / waitForFunction)
 *  - the "connecting" indicator is captured with a MutationObserver installed
 *    by addInitScript before any page script runs, so it cannot be missed
 *  - the dice has an infinite bob animation (.can) → clicked with {force:true}
 */
const { chromium } = require('playwright');
const fs = require('fs');

const BASE = (process.argv[2] || 'http://localhost:8131').replace(/\/$/, '');
const EXECUTABLE = '/home/sarthak/.cache/ms-playwright/chromium-1148/chrome-linux/chrome';
const VIEWPORT = { width: 430, height: 900 }; // mobile
const BUDGET_MS = 240000; // ~3 min per-script budget (+ margin)

const ORDER = [
  'configured', 'connecting', 'roomCode', 'joinWorked', 'realtimeSync',
  'boardRendered', 'tokenChips', 'rollSynced', 'pageErrors',
];

{

The demo board\n * works without Firebase, so nothing is written to the project's live Firestore.\n */\nimport { createServer } from \"node:http\";\nimport { readFile } from \"node:fs/promises\";\nimport { extname, join, normalize } from \"node:path\";\nimport { chromium } from \"playwright-core\";\n\nconst SOURCE = \"/home/sarthak/Desktop/Projects/ludo-vercel\";\nconst OUT_DIR = new URL(\"../public/projects/ludo/\", import.meta.url).pathname;\nconst EXECUTABLE =\n  \"/home/sarthak/.cache/ms-playwright/chromium-1148/chrome-linux/chrome\";\nconst PORT = 8137;\nconst VIEWPORT = { width: 1440, height: 900 };\n\nconst MIME = {\n  \".html\": \"text/html; charset=utf-8\",\n  \".js\": \"text/javascript; charset=utf-8\",\n  \".mjs\": \"text/javascript; charset=utf-8\",\n  \".css\": \"text/css; charset=utf-8\",\n  \".json\": \"application/json; charset=utf-8\",\n  \".webmanifest\": \"application/manifest+json; charset=utf-8\",\n  \".png\": \"image/png\",\n  \".svg\": \"image/svg+xml\",\n  \".ico\": \"image/x-icon\",\n  \".map\": \"application/json; charset=utf-8\",\n  \".woff2\": \"font/woff2\",\n};\n\nconst server = createServer(async (req, res) => {\n  try {\n    const url = new URL(req.url ?? \"/\", `http://localhost:${PORT}`);\n    const rel = normalize(decodeURIComponent(url.pathname)).replace(\n      /^(\\.\\.[/\\\\])+/,\n      \"\",\n    );\n    const filePath = join(SOURCE, rel === \"/\" ? \"index.html\" : rel);\n    const body = await readFile(filePath);\n    res.writeHead(200, {\n      \"Content-Type\": MIME[extname(filePath)] ?? \"application/octet-stream\",\n      \"Cache-Control\": \"no-store\",\n    });\n    res.end(body);\n  } catch {\n    res.writeHead(404, { \"Content-Type\": \"text/plain\" });\n    res.end(\"not found\");\n  }\n});\n\nawait new Promise((resolve) => server.listen(PORT, resolve));\nconsole.log(`serving ${SOURCE} on :${PORT}`);\n\nconst browser = await chromium.launch({\n  executablePath: EXECUTABLE,\n  args: [\"--no-sandbox\", \"--disable-dev-shm-usage\"],\n});\n\ntry {\n  const page = await browser.newPage({ viewport: VIEWPORT, deviceScaleFactor: 2 });\n  page.on(\"pageerror\", (err) => console.log(\"pageerror:\", err.message));\n\n  await page.goto(`http://localhost:${PORT}/`, { waitUntil: \"networkidle\" });\n  await page.waitForTimeout(2500);\n  await page.screenshot({ path: join(OUT_DIR, \"home.png\") });\n  console.log(\"captured home.png\");\n\n  const demo = page.getByText(\"Watch a 5-player demo\", { exact: false }).first();\n  if (await demo.count()) {\n    await demo.click({ force: true });\n    await page.waitForTimeout(4000);\n    await page.screenshot({ path: join(OUT_DIR, \"demo-board.png\") });\n    console.log(\"captured demo-board.png\");\n  } else {\n    console.log(\"demo button not found — skipped demo-board.png\");\n  }\n} finally {\n  await browser.close();\n  server.close();\n}\n",

### Result 1
- **Category:** Animation
- **Issue:** Reduced Motion
- **Platform:** All
- **Description:** Respect user's motion preferences
- **Do:** Check prefers-reduced-motion media query
- **Don't:** Ignore accessibility motion settings
- **Code Example Good:** @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)
- **Code Example Bad:** No motion query check
- **Severity:** High

### Result 2
- **Category:** Accessibility
- **Issue:** Motion Sensitivity
- **Platform:** All
- **Description:** Parallax/Scroll-jacking causes nausea
- **Do:** Honor prefers-reduced-motion and present the final readable state without parallax or scroll-jacking
- **Don't:** Force scroll effects
- **Code Example Good:** @media (prefers-reduced-motion)
- **Code Example Bad:** ScrollTrigger.create()
- **Severity:** High

### Result 3
- **Category:** Animation
- **Issue:** Excessive Motion
- **Platform:** All
- **Description:** Too many animations cause distraction and motion sickness
- **Do:** Animate 1-2 key elements per view maximum
- **Don't:** Animate everything that moves
- **Code Example Good:** Single hero animation
- **Code Example Bad:** animate-bounce on 5+ elements
- **Severity:** High

### Result 4
- **Category:** Animation
- **Issue:** Duration Timing
- **Platform:** All
- **Description:** Motion duration depends on distance complexity platform and user context
- **Do:** Use shared motion tokens and test that feedback stays responsive
- **Don't:** Present 150-300ms or any cutoff as a universal requirement
- **Code Example Good:** transition-colors duration-200
- **Code Example Bad:** One duration copied to every transition
- **Severity:** Medium

Reduced-motion users get\n * the final number immediately — the information never depends on the animation.\n */\nexport function Counter({\n  value,\n  duration = 1.6,\n  suffix = \"\",\n  className,\n}: {\n  value: number;\n  duration?: number;\n  suffix?: string;\n  className?: string;\n}) {\n  const ref = useRef<HTMLSpanElement>(null);\n  const inView = useInView(ref, { once: true, margin: \"0px 0px -15% 0px\" });\n  const reduced = useReducedMotion();\n  const mv = useMotionValue(0);\n  const text = useTransform(mv, (v) => `${Math.round(v)}`);\n\n  useEffect(() => {\n    if (!inView) return;\n    if (reduced) {\n      mv.set(value);\n      return;\n    }\n    const controls = animate(mv, value, {\n      duration,\n      ease: [0.16, 1, 0.3, 1],\n    });\n    return () => controls.stop();\n  }, [inView, value, duration, mv, reduced]);\n\n  return (\n    <span ref={ref} className={cn(\"tabular\", className)}>\n      <motion.span>{text}</motion.span>\n      {suffix}\n    </span>\n  );\n}\n",

Desktop pointers only. */\nexport function Magnetic({\n  children,\n  className,\n  strength = 0.35,\n}: {\n  children: ReactNode;\n  className?: string;\n  strength?: number;\n}) {\n  const ref = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);\n  const reduced = useReducedMotion();\n  const x = useMotionValue(0);\n  const y = useMotionValue(0);\n  const sx = useSpring(x, { stiffness: 220, damping: 18, mass: 0.4 });\n  const sy = useSpring(y, { stiffness: 220, damping: 18, mass: 0.4 });\n\n  const onMove = (event: React.PointerEvent) => {\n    if (reduced || event.pointerType !== \"mouse\" || !ref.current) return;\n    const rect = ref.current.getBoundingClientRect();\n    x.set((event.clientX - (rect.left + rect.width / 2)) * strength);\n    y.set((event.clientY - (rect.top + rect.height / 2)) * strength);\n  };\n\n  const reset = () => {\n    x.set(0);\n    y.set(0);\n  };\n\n  return (\n    <motion.div\n      ref={ref}\n      style={{ x: sx, y: sy }}\n      onPointerMove={onMove}\n      onPointerLeave={reset}\n      className={cn(\"inline-block\", className)}\n    >\n      {children}\n    </motion.div>\n  );\n}\n\n/** Cards that tilt toward the pointer — keeps 3D transforms off layout. */\nexport function Tilt({\n  children,\n  className,\n  max = 7,\n}: {\n  children: ReactNode;\n  className?: string;\n  max?: number;\n}) {\n  const ref = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);\n  const reduced = useReducedMotion();\n  const rx = useMotionValue(0);\n  const ry = useMotionValue(0);\n  const srx = useSpring(rx, { stiffness: 180, damping: 20, mass: 0.5 });\n  const sry = useSpring(ry, { stiffness: 180, damping: 20, mass: 0.5 });\n\n  const onMove = (event: React.PointerEvent) => {\n    if (reduced || event.pointerType !== \"mouse\" || !ref.current) return;\n    const rect = ref.current.getBoundingClientRect();\n    const px = (event.clientX - rect.left) / rect.width - 0.5;\n    const py = (event.clientY - rect.top) / rect.height - 0.5;\n    ry.set(px * max);\n    rx.set(-py * max);\n  };\n\n  const reset = () => {\n    rx.set(0);\n    ry.set(0);\n  };\n\n  return (\n    <motion.div\n      ref={ref}\n      style={{ rotateX: srx, rotateY: sry, transformPerspective: 900 }}\n      onPointerMove={onMove}\n      onPointerLeave={reset}\n      className={cn(\"will-change-transform\", className)}\n    >\n      {children}\n    </motion.div>\n  );\n}\n",

Disabled for touch pointers and reduced motion.\n */\nexport function CustomCursor() {\n  const [enabled, setEnabled] = useState(false);\n  const [active, setActive] = useState(false);\n  const reduced = useReducedMotion();\n\n  const x = useMotionValue(-100);\n  const y = useMotionValue(-100);\n  const ringX = useSpring(x, { stiffness: 340, damping: 32, mass: 0.35 });\n  const ringY = useSpring(y, { stiffness: 340, damping: 32, mass: 0.35 });\n\n  useEffect(() => {\n    const fine = window.matchMedia(\"(pointer: fine)\").matches;\n    const noMotion = window.matchMedia(\"(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)\").matches;\n    if (!fine || noMotion) return;\n\n    setEnabled(true);\n    document.documentElement.classList.add(\"has-custom-cursor\");\n\n    const move = (event: PointerEvent) => {\n      x.set(event.clientX);\n      y.set(event.clientY);\n      const target = event.target as HTMLElement | null;\n      setActive(Boolean(target?.closest(\"a, button, [role='button'], input, textarea, [data-cursor]\")));\n    };\n\n    window.addEventListener(\"pointermove\", move, { passive: true });\n    return () => {\n      window.removeEventListener(\"pointermove\", move);\n      document.documentElement.classList.remove(\"has-custom-cursor\");\n    };\n  }, [x, y]);\n\n  if (!enabled || reduced) return null;\n\n  return (\n    <>\n      <motion.div\n        aria-hidden\n        style={{ x, y }}\n        className=\"pointer-events-none fixed left-0 top-0 z-[120] h-2 w-2 -translate-x-1/2 -translate-y-1/2 rounded-full bg-[var(--color-accent-2)]\"\n      />\n      <motion.div\n        aria-hidden\n        style={{ x: ringX, y: ringY }}\n        animate={{ scale: active ? 2.1 : 1, opacity: active ? 0.55 : 0.35 }}\n        transition={{ duration: 0.25, ease: [0.16, 1, 0.3, 1] }}\n        className=\"pointer-events-none fixed left-0 top-0 z-[119] h-8 w-8 -translate-x-1/2 -translate-y-1/2 rounded-full border border-[var(--color-accent-2)]\"\n      />\n    </>\n  );\n}\n",
  "filePath": "/home/sarthak/Desktop/Projects/portfolio/src/components/motion/custom-cursor.tsx"
}

  "content": "\"use client\";\n\nimport { MotionConfig } from \"motion/react\";\nimport { useEffect, type ReactNode } from \"react\";\nimport Lenis from \"lenis\";\n\n/**\n * Motion defaults + Lenis smooth scrolling.\n * Lenis is skipped for touch pointers and reduced-motion users, who get the\n * browser's native scroll behaviour.\n */\nexport function Providers({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {\n  useEffect(() => {\n    const fine = window.matchMedia(\"(pointer: fine)\").matches;\n    const reduced = window.matchMedia(\"(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)\").matches;\n    if (!fine || reduced) return;\n\n    const lenis = new Lenis({\n      duration: 1.05,\n      easing: (t: number) => Math.min(1, 1.001 - Math.pow(2, -10 * t)),\n      smoothWheel: true,\n      touchMultiplier: 1.5,\n    });\n\n    document.documentElement.classList.add(\"lenis\");\n\n    let frame = 0;\n    const raf = (time: number) => {\n      lenis.raf(time);\n      frame = requestAnimationFrame(raf);\n    };\n    frame = requestAnimationFrame(raf);\n\n    return () => {\n      cancelAnimationFrame(frame);\n      document.documentElement.classList.remove(\"lenis\");\n      lenis.destroy();\n    };\n  }, []);\n\n  return <MotionConfig reducedMotion=\"user\">{children}</MotionConfig>;\n}\n",

}
